# Held-out year evaluation: report

Generated by `scripts/analyze_evaluation.py` under `docs/EVALUATION_PLAN_20261114.md`. Aggregates only.

Opened 2026-11-15T12:17:29.926458+00:00 at freeze commit `171a918964f101b5958bac286bcdbaf37a920fa5`.

## Limits that go with every number

- Not an untouched test. The year 2022-05-01 to 2023-05-01 was scored on November 7, 2026 with an earlier pipeline, and parts of it served as validation and test in older work. Within this campaign every model's training job (data, recipe, seed and length), every rule and every definition was fixed before the year was opened. The year was opened once. Passes over each gauge's file in this opening: Jefferson 1; Galax 1; Sugar Grove 1. The direction of C1 to C3 was foreseeable from the November 7 numbers: a verdict of held up on them confirms a direction already seen once with other models.
- Refit length is a convention, not a tested optimum: each refit trains for its existing twin's validation-selected number of epochs on a longer role, with no validation check of its own. The first-year loss scale differs from the fit-only scale by +1.2% at Jefferson, +18.2% at Galax and -1.6% at Sugar Grove, so a refit's normalized loss differs from its twin's, most at Galax.
- Recipes were chosen on full-input validation scores at seeds 1729 and 1730. Removed-input variants use a recipe that was not tuned for them. Seed 1731 took no part in choosing recipes.
- Three seeds per cell at most; every table states the seeds it used. Intervals resample weeks with the fitted models held fixed; they do not include the variation between training runs.
- Scores are conditional on origins with complete inputs (Jefferson 8107 of 8574; Galax 7988 of 8574; Sugar Grove 8185 of 8574 origins in support). Gaps in the gauge record can coincide with floods, so the scored cells may under-represent them.
- Intervals are nominal-95% percentile intervals from a paired block bootstrap, pointwise, with no adjustment for the number of comparisons. Their calibration on these blocks is printed below. It was measured on pairs of network forecasts with blocks resampled independently, the favourable case, not on the comparisons the verdicts use. An interval that excludes zero is not called significant, and verdicts are not adjusted for their number (about 36 per model set).
- One year at three gauges of one region. The gauges share storms, so agreement across gauges is not three independent confirmations. Squared-error statistics can rest on a few high-flow periods: each headline row shows the 7-day block with the largest share of the squared error and the range when single blocks are left out. The November 7 record places the year's largest flows on November 11 and 12, 2022; the author knew that date before the opening.
- Most of the year uses NWM v2.2 (from 2022-06-28T12Z); all training data are v2.1. Version and season are confounded.
- Retrospective archive inputs. Gauge readings up to the origin are used as archived; whether they were available at issue time was not established. No real-time claim.
- Removal effects measure the predictive value of an input to these models on these cases, at fixed capacity. They are not causal importance, and no_nwm_predictor still uses NWM as the residual anchor.
- Counts over the 270 gauge, lead and metric cells are dependent and descriptive. No architecture is ranked.

### Claims and verdicts

Rules: plan section 10 as replaced by Amendments 1 and 2. A verdict needs all three seeds and comes from the 7-day interval of their mean. Beside it, without changing it: the verdict each other block length would give, the range of the estimate when each 7-day block is left out, and the same statistic from the validation analysis of the existing models. A verdict follows the interval: a cell can read held up while its estimate lies outside its own interval.

C3 and C6 are judged differently on purpose. C6 reads held up whenever its interval includes zero, however wide: that reproduces the validation finding, which was itself "not distinguishable", and does not show that networks and persistence are equal. C3 reads consistent with no effect only when its interval lies within plus or minus 10%. That margin is wide, about ten times the validation effect at Sugar Grove: a gain of 8% over NWM with a narrow interval would still read consistent with no effect. The estimate and interval are printed beside every verdict.

**C9. Single weather fields show no consistent effect** (descriptive, no verdict). Of 36 entries, 36 have three seeds: 8 intervals exclude zero (3.7 expected by chance from resampling weeks alone; the rate is measured by resampling weeks for fixed pairs of forecasts; a removed-input fit and its full-input fit are separate training runs, so more exclusions than this can arise without any input effect (validation: 9 of 48 no-effect entries)); 8 have the same sign in all three seeds (9.0 expected by chance); largest effect in absolute value 7.2%.

**C10. At Sugar Grove no input removal matters** (descriptive, no verdict). Of 24 entries, 24 have three seeds: 4 intervals exclude zero (2.4 expected by chance from resampling weeks alone; the rate is measured by resampling weeks for fixed pairs of forecasts; a removed-input fit and its full-input fit are separate training runs, so more exclusions than this can arise without any input effect (validation: 9 of 48 no-effect entries)); 17 have the same sign in all three seeds (6.0 expected by chance); largest effect in absolute value 4.8%.

### Claims and verdicts

Rules: plan section 10 as replaced by Amendments 1 and 2. A verdict needs all three seeds and comes from the 7-day interval of their mean. Beside it, without changing it: the verdict each other block length would give, the range of the estimate when each 7-day block is left out, and the same statistic from the validation analysis of the existing models. A verdict follows the interval: a cell can read held up while its estimate lies outside its own interval.

C3 and C6 are judged differently on purpose. C6 reads held up whenever its interval includes zero, however wide: that reproduces the validation finding, which was itself "not distinguishable", and does not show that networks and persistence are equal. C3 reads consistent with no effect only when its interval lies within plus or minus 10%. That margin is wide, about ten times the validation effect at Sugar Grove: a gain of 8% over NWM with a narrow interval would still read consistent with no effect. The estimate and interval are printed beside every verdict.

**C9. Single weather fields show no consistent effect** (descriptive, no verdict). Of 36 entries, 36 have three seeds: 8 intervals exclude zero (3.7 expected by chance from resampling weeks alone; the rate is measured by resampling weeks for fixed pairs of forecasts; a removed-input fit and its full-input fit are separate training runs, so more exclusions than this can arise without any input effect (validation: 9 of 48 no-effect entries)); 7 have the same sign in all three seeds (9.0 expected by chance); largest effect in absolute value 14.4%.

**C10. At Sugar Grove no input removal matters** (descriptive, no verdict). Of 24 entries, 24 have three seeds: 6 intervals exclude zero (2.4 expected by chance from resampling weeks alone; the rate is measured by resampling weeks for fixed pairs of forecasts; a removed-input fit and its full-input fit are separate training runs, so more exclusions than this can arise without any input effect (validation: 9 of 48 no-effect entries)); 9 have the same sign in all three seeds (6.0 expected by chance); largest effect in absolute value 3.3%.

## Dispositions and checks

- refit: planned 243; 243 scored.
- existing: planned 243; 243 scored.
- Every scored output was checked against its model's record, the cached batches and the scoring code: 243 existing, 243 refit.
- Worst difference between recomputed and scorer per-lead metrics: existing 2.4e-14, refit 2.0e-14.
- All models and simple corrections at a gauge share identical scored cells, labels and NWM values.
- Persistence donors: Jefferson 8107 eligible_original_observation; Galax 7988 eligible_original_observation; Sugar Grove 8185 eligible_original_observation.
